ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u33	The next item is the debate on motions 1-00085 (text 3), tabled by Senator Ferrazzi and other Senators, 1-00097, tabled by Senator Gallone and other Senators, 1-00122, tabled by Senator De Petris and other Senators, and 1-00135, tabled by Senator L'Abbate and other Senators, on combating climate change. ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u34	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, today is the World Climate Day, particularly dedicated to climate pollution, air pollution and climate crisis. Our planet is facing profound climate change and, in the absence of concrete action, these phenomena could lead us within a few years to a point of no return. The Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change has repeatedly outlined the situation and warned about the irreversible effects of climate change, calling on States to take urgent decisions. The changes that are taking place also concern our country - as has been dramatically seen in the devastations of autumn 2018, in many ways unprecedented - and they no longer make only mitigation policies sufficient, and therefore they also require adaptation policies both in the territories and in the cities. According to the Intergovernmental Group, there is a close relationship between human activity and climate change. With the current rate by 2030, the global average temperature is likely to rise by 1.5 degrees Celsius. This increase would result in the dissolution of the permafrost, the raising of the seas, the disappearance of large coastal areas, the spread of infectious diseases, the emergence of new pathologies, as well as ecosystem damage to forests and wetlands, the increase of desertification and the reduction of drinking water available. The response must be immediate and cannot compromise the need to progressively reduce greenhouse gas emissions. At the Paris Climate Conference in December 2015, 195 countries adopted the first universal and legally binding agreement on the global climate. The Agreement defined a global action plan not to exceed the average temperature increase of one and a half degrees Celsius and set the objective of limiting the increase in global temperature well below two degrees Celsius. Although important in its intentions, the Agreement has so far not produced adequate global measures to implement the commitments it has made and therefore to be decisive in order to intervene and reverse the current trend. [[Brusio. Call the president back.]] On 27 May, the Pontifical Academy of Sciences organized an international conference entitled "Climate change and new evidence from science, engineering, and policy". On that occasion the Pope spoke with a report on a common plan for the survival of the planet. In the speeches of all the scientists present, regarding the agreements on the UN's Sustainable Development Goals and those on the climate at the COP21 conference in Paris, it has emerged that in reality, even this year and in the previous two years, investments on fossil fuels continue to grow. Meanwhile, Mr President, the International Energy Agency has recently shown that investments in clean energy have decreased for the second consecutive year. The result - to say one - is that the concentration of carbon dioxide, among the major causes of global warming, has reached 415 parts per million, the highest level ever achieved on our planet. An important step in the fight against global climate change will therefore be, within the EU, to approve, in the final version, by December this year, the integrated national energy and climate plan. In a separate legislative resolution, the European Parliament agreed that in 2030 the share of renewable energy must be 35% of the European Union's energy consumption. The document - as provided for in Regulation 2018/1999/EU on the governance of the Energy Union - will be the subject of a wide-ranging discussion in Brussels in recent months in response to the draft plans sent by the Member States. Italy on that occasion will have the opportunity to update, refine and improve targets and objectives to make them uniform to those laid down in the plan approved by the European Parliament and the Paris Agreement. As far as our country is concerned, grasping the centrality of the climate crisis means first of all accelerating the energy transition towards the use of renewable sources and energy efficiency with a gradual overcoming of fossil fuels. The process of decarbonisation that requires demanding interventions, supported by progressive carbon tax measures to be combined with mechanisms of compensation and protection of both competitiveness (the border tax) and social sustainability, and with a gradual reallocation of existing subsidies harmful to the environment and territories, must be pursued with momentum. At the same time, Mr President, it is necessary and urgent to launch a plan to adapt to climate change. Mitigation, i.e. emission reduction, is no longer sufficient, because the climate is now structurally modified. It is therefore necessary to adopt a policy for territories that revisits and reinforces policies to prevent and mitigate risks and damage caused, for example, by landslides and floods. Hydrogeological failure must be addressed with land management that takes into account the new climate environment, so that risks and damage can be prevented and mitigated. In this context, particular attention should be paid to the themes of urban regeneration and to more incisive rules on soil consumption, as well as to all interventions, in an infrastructural logic, restoration of habitats and hydrographic networks. Mr President, it is quite clear that cities are and will be increasingly central in terms of demographics and development and, therefore, will be, for these reasons too, the most energetic and polluting places. Special attention will therefore be essential in them and for them. The management of water, due to the increasing rainfall of floods and the persistent drought phases, must be able to rely on timely legislation that, by reaffirming the public nature of the water, allows economies of scale, ensures homogeneous quality and guarantees security of supply. Now there is no time to extend, but in the text that we will of course deliver a definition is specified on the basis of tariffs. Water scarcity and crop crisis are just some of the immediate effects that the current climate crisis generates, especially in some parts of the world, where countless people are reduced to famine and are driven towards mass migration, which, in the long term, take on an uncontrollable scale. The Nairobi Assembly last month spoke of climate migration as a traumatic event of the present and the future. The green economy, President, is not a sectoral issue for sensitive to the issue, but a real integrated approach, capable of both a comprehensive vision and targeted interventions throughout the production and consumption process. The circular economy is a fundamental pillar of the green economy. More must be done, without further delay and hesitation, and action must be swift, decisive and joint. There is a collective and moral obligation for future generations to do everything humanly possible to stop climate change and to respond to their dramatic effects. Mr President, I shall finish. Italy can take a leading role in this process in the world. For all these reasons, President and senators, we ask to declare the state of environmental and climatic emergency of the country and to work, together with Parliament, to reach a change of direction in all sectors of our economy, so as to allow, in rapid and certain times, in compliance with scientific indications and international agreements, the necessary energy transition that pushes our country towards the reduction of polluting emissions into the atmosphere and the progressive decarbonization of the economy. [[Applause from the PD Group]]
ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u35	I call Senator Berutti to explain motion No 97.
ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u36	Mr President, the Paris Agreement, approved on 12 December 2015 at the 21st session of the Conference of the Parties to the Climate Convention (COP21), was an important step forward in a still very long and rough path to counter global warming. Within the framework of the United Nations Convention on Climate Change (UNFCCC), the Agreement included elements for a progressive reduction of global greenhouse gas emissions and was based, for the first time, on common principles valid for all countries without distinction between industrialised countries and developing countries. One of the main objectives was to direct private and state financial flows towards low-emission greenhouse gas development and to improve adaptation capacity to climate change. Following this important stage, the Marrakech Conference in 2016 (COP22), the Bonn Conference in 2017 (COP23) and last, in December 2018, the Katowice Conference (COP24), in which rules were established to put into practice by 2020 what was decided at COP21, the Paris Climate Conference in 2015. In particular, the criteria for measuring carbon dioxide emissions and assessing measures to combat climate change in individual countries have been decided. The Conference was attended by representatives of 196 countries, including the United States, despite the fact that President Donald Trump withdrew the United States of America from the Paris Agreement. The main contrast that emerged during the Conference was the latest report of the United Nations Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC), which deals with the scientific analysis of climate trends and the production of models on its evolution. In the report, the IPCC confirmed that an average global temperature increase of at least 1.5 degrees Celsius on pre-industrial levels is now inevitable - it will take place over the next twelve years - and that in order to keep within three degrees Celsius of overall increase it will be necessary to cut carbon dioxide emissions by 45% by 2020. In the absence of radical actions, the average temperature will increase beyond two degrees Celsius leading to more extreme climatic events and changing the climate of entire geographical areas, with consequences for millions of people. Although two of the largest countries in the world (the United States and Russia) have expressed considerable concerns about the choices to be made in relation to the future of the planet, technical decisions have been taken on how the different countries, depending on their level of development, will have to reduce their carbon dioxide emissions. Climate change, as a cause and multiplier of risks, is an important challenge for humanity and all countries and actors at global level must do their best to combat them through individual incisive actions. In this context, Italy's commitments to major international events have always been clear and clear in its desire to contribute to an improvement in climate and environmental conditions. In fact, it is recent news that the European Commission has referred our country to the Court of Justice of the European Union for the repeated violation of the annual limits of nitrogen dioxide in the air of the cities and for the failure to adapt to the EU rules of waste water treatment systems of more than 700 agglomerations and 30 sensitive areas from the environmental point of view. With this in mind, we must bear in mind that, without profoundly changing the current production system, global warming cannot be mitigated. It goes without saying that the production system is modified only by upstream measures, firstly by a new energy policy that favours the use of low-carbon technologies and energy sources and defines a real decarbonisation road map covering all sectors, through public investment, tax incentives and simplification. It is clear that fossil fuels are a problem today and in a few decades' time they must be exceeded, compared to today's simple reduction, in order to respect the emission levels that have been decided in Paris. These are land planning choices aimed at promoting economic development in a sustainable way, as an alternative to a fossil fuel-based model and on which the current Government has a duty to give clear and coherent signals. Another major issue on which it is important to pay attention is a new environmental tax as an imperative for future economic policies. Only in this way can Italy fully fit into the European process designed with the new directive on the circular economy, moving taxation from work to pollution of production processes and products after and during their use. A key chapter also concerns environmental taxation in the field of goods and products. In this context, the direction is to review value added tax, with the aim of directing the market towards sustainable modes of production and consumption, including, for example, a reduced tax regime for manufactured goods made with a percentage of recycled material, i.e. shifting taxation from work to pollution. On 8 January 2019, the proposal for an Integrated National Plan for Energy and Climate sent to Brussels by the Ministry of Economic Development in conjunction with the Ministry of the Environment and Protection of the Territory and the Sea and the Ministry of Infrastructure and Transport was announced. As provided for in the Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of the European Union, the document will be discussed at European level in the coming months to arrive at a final version by the end of 2019. The Plan contains the energy and climate targets that Member States undertake to achieve by 2030. The document should also indicate the instruments (policies, measures and their economic cover) through which - credibly - these objectives are to be achieved. To seize this possibility does not mean to slow down the infrastructural and technological process - as many would like - but rather a dynamic commitment aimed at conceiving the investments in great works, such as the high-speed train and the modernization of the railway network, more competitive and more convenient, from the point of view of the environmental impact, of the road transport, especially with regard to the goods. The aim must be to achieve a safe, economical, efficient and sustainable energy; an expanding and, at the same time, increasingly decarbonised economy; a neutral approach to all energy sources, starting from an analysis of the entire life cycle and rewarding sources that can actually ensure the greatest benefits for the environment, for the health of our citizens and for the economy of our country. For each kilogram of resource consumed our country generates (at purchasing power parity) 4 euros of GDP, against a European average of 2.2 and values between 2.3 and 3.6 of all the other major continental economies, as estimated by the Institute of Environmental Research Italy. Ours is the European country with the highest percentage of recycling on all waste (urban, industrial and so on, including mining) and this also thanks to innovative methods of waste management and advanced systems for their recovery. Several case-law judgments, last of which the decision of the Council of State n. 1229/2018, have provided a very restrictive interpretation in relation to the possibility for the competent authority (region or province by this delegate) to assess the existence of the conditions laid down in the norm. To stop this virtuous process, also through the failure to allow the Regions to define the criteria for the cessation of the qualification of refusal by case, casts a shadow of uncertainty on the numerous integrated ordinary authorisations that today enable the recovery of waste not regulated at Community and ministerial level. We therefore call on the Government to undertake to take responsibility, among the countries participating in the United Nations Climate Conference, for the adoption of a code requiring a high level of transparency, with robust rules binding on all parties in order to accurately measure progress and strengthen confidence among the parties involved in the international process. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. I invite you to conclude, Senator. Mr Berutti (FI-BP). Just a few more minutes, ma'am. PRESIDENT. - The debate is closed. One minute, Senator Something's a little complicated. Please continue. Mr Berutti (FI-BP). Thank you. The Government is also committed to taking urgent action to reduce the limits of nitrogen dioxide, including not to incur infringement proceedings by the European Union. We also call on the Government to commit itself to propose as a matter of urgency a transitional rule in the interests of the implementation of the directive, which allows installations currently built but stationary to operate regularly in order to avoid the allocation of large quantities of waste to landfills; to ensure the financial autonomy of the local authorities that commit the resources deriving from taxation to enterprises; to provide for a public investment plan aimed at: promoting a new energy-environmental model; to equip public buildings, starting from schools, with photovoltaic systems and energy efficiency; to ensure the completion of capacity marketing, aimed at more diversification of sources of supply and support for renewable and programmable hydroelectric sources at the same time. [[Applause from the FI-BP Group.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u37	On behalf of the Assembly, I greet the teachers and students of the Institute of Higher Education Giovan Battista Odierna, of Palma di Montechiaro, in the province of Agrigento, who are attending our work. [[Applause.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u38	Mr President, today - as many have said - is the world day for the environment and I think we should all make an effort to get out of the celebration ritual. Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the time has come for decisions: we can no longer ignore anything, because the evidence of climate change and the threats to the human species they produce is now a fact acquired by the scientific community. I would like to recall the data quickly. In the last report on climate warming, published in October 2018 by the Intergovernmental Committee on Climate Change (IPCC), it is clear that limiting warming to 1.5 degrees requires rapid decisions and urgent, immediate and forward-looking changes in all aspects of society. These changes are absolutely necessary because we are at a point where there is a risk of no return. We're practically past that already. Being already in 2019, we have eleven years to be able to quickly contain the increase in temperature. The Paris Agreement - certainly an important step forward - did not produce those changes, however, with the assumption of responsibility on the part of individual countries, which make us hope to take decisions on urgent changes, on a transition, especially energy, and a real industrial revolution, absolutely necessary. Unfortunately, the recent Climate Conference held in Katowice in Poland in December 2018 also confirmed a lack of effectiveness in making binding commitments. I think it is absolutely necessary to realize another issue which, especially in our country and in Europe, is not considered in its entirety, which I would like to recall very quickly. There is a very dangerous trend observed in the Mediterranean area. We have been accustomed to hearing in the narration that climate change can have a more dramatic effect maybe outside this area and, instead, it is precisely in the Mediterranean area that it has been observed not generically, but with scientific data, widely confirmed also by our National Research Council, the greatest increase. We have seen in the Mediterranean area an increase of 1,58, far above - dangerously above - the average temperature increase of one degree. I therefore believe, to an even greater extent, that Italy must not only be the protagonist of an effective campaign and initiative in Europe to speed up emissions reduction targets, but also take decisions. The motion that we are presenting today proposes the declaration of the state of climate and environmental emergency, because it is necessary to do things quickly, not only go to the demonstrations or receive - as we did in the Senate - Greta Thunberg. We need to act immediately to start the real processes to speed up the energy transition. The first question is as follows: we have to end fossil subsidies. [[Applause from the Group of the European People's Party (Christian Democrats) and ……]] , and not just because we need those resources to invest them in the energy transition, renewables and an accelerated sustainability plan. We must do this because this means not calling into question and shutting down once and for all with the fossil economy. We no longer have time: we must speed up and anticipate a plan of adaptation. ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u39	I call Senator L'Abbate to explain motion No 135.
ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u40	Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, I shall be presenting motion No 135, which will have a rewording in commitments 2 and 8, the text of which I will deliver after the Presidency. I would point out that in 1988 the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) was set up, an international scientific forum for the study and evaluation of climate change established by the World Weather Organisation (WMO) and the United Nations Environment Programme (UNEP), with the aim of providing a scientific vision on the state of knowledge of climate change and its potential environmental and socio-economic impacts. Since its first evaluation report in 1990, the IPCC has revealed that carbon dioxide and other climatic gases such as nitrous oxide, methane and chlorofluorocarbons contribute to increasing the natural greenhouse effect and that human activities are among the main causes of the increase in the concentration of greenhouse gases in the atmosphere. In 2018, NASA and the U.S. Air and Ocean Research Agency stated that the previous five years have been the hottest years of the last century. According to data collected and analysed by the Institute of Atmospheric Sciences and Climate of the National Research Council, 2018 was the hottest year ever recorded in Italy since 1800, with an anomaly above the average of 1.58 degrees, compared to the reference period from 1971 to 2000. The negative environmental impact of climate-altering emissions generated by human activities contributes to global warming, so-called global warming, and has as a consequence the increase in intensity and frequency of extreme weather phenomena, such as excessively high or extremely rigid temperatures - as happened in the last month - especially off-season, snowed at low altitude, exceptionally strong winds, water bombs and intense hailstorms, alternated with periods of severe drought. With the increase in extreme climate phenomena, the risks of hydrogeological disruption and, therefore, damage to our agriculture, infrastructure and territory are also increasing; these are impacts that cause insecurity in our cities and in terms of the health of our citizens. Currently, also the costs related to the hydrogeological failure of the Italian territory are high: it is estimated about 2.5 billion euros per year, but the figure could increase over the next decades, as well as the costs related to the containment of the fires and the safety of the territories. The increase in the average temperature at global level leads to the melting of glaciers, causing a rise in the sea level, which could reach particularly severe values within a few decades, with disastrous impacts in particular for the island regions and for those territories that have developed their economy and urban settlements on the coasts. In October 2018, the Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change (IPCC) presented its special report which, for the first time, assessed the impacts of climate change on the target of 1.5 degrees Celsius global temperature increase. The document has shown that global warming has already increased by one degree centigrade compared to pre-industrial levels and that it is growing by approximately 0.2 degrees centigrade to decade. At the last Conference of the Parties, held in December 2018 in Katowice, Poland, what is called the facilitative dialogue to promote new emission reduction commitments was launched. The conference concluded with the approval of the Operational Manual for the implementation of the Paris Agreement. The operational manual (rulebook) established, inter alia, the use of the new guidelines in the assessment of emissions emissions from climate change and the preparation by Member States of an emission inventory, with a two-year deadline. It must be considered that the Italian Government has developed a proposal for a fundamental instrument for the energy and environmental policy of our country for the next ten years, such as the Integrated National Energy and Climate Plan (PNIEC) submitted to public consultation since 19 March 2019. In addition, in April last year, the European Parliament made provision for a regulation which, for the seven-year period 2021-2027, the European Regional Development Fund and the Cohesion Fund cannot finance investments linked to polluting fuels such as oil and hydrocarbons. It is believed that being able to mitigate the effects of climate change - in particular the consequent impacts on the natural, humanized and urbanized environment - and to govern its causes is not only a moral duty towards future generations (which they have manifested every Friday in our squares), but also a strategic priority for the Italian national economy. For these reasons, on World Environment Day we ask the Government to make some commitments. We call for every initiative to decarbonise the economy within the framework of our own competences, while ensuring the security of the country's energy system, setting the European Union's long-term strategy for reducing greenhouse gas emissions as an objective. I refer to Communication COM (2018) 773 of 28 November 2018. In addition, we call for the implementation of any measure that favours the transition from fossil energy sources to renewable sources and from the linear economy to the circular economy, encouraging investment in research and eco-innovation. We also call for the development of eco-efficient production systems, using bio-economy and eco-design. Moreover, in order to reduce energy waste, we intend to commit the Government to further strengthen the path of energy eco-efficiency to be applied to public and private assets, including through synergies with the productive world for constant training of operators in all sectors. It also commits the Government to put in place every initiative aimed at promoting distributed self-production of energy from fossil sources (so the consumer is also producer, a prosumer); to promote, in synergy with local authorities, awareness-raising and information campaigns aimed at citizens, on good environmental practices aimed at the mitigation of climate change, including through the introduction of environmental education in schools of all degrees; to promote infrastructure development policies and interventions aimed at promoting virtuous initiatives of urban and extra-urban sustainable mobility, including intermodal transport for the direct benefit of the environment and ecosystems, but also of human health and well-being; to promote, in agreement with the regions and local authorities and the district basin authorities, within their competences, interventions aimed at the prevention of hydrogeological risk, as well as measures for the responsible use of soil; to implement, within their own competences, all the measures necessary to achieve the objectives of greenhouse gas reduction agreed at international and European level (in particular with the so-called Paris agreement), taking into account of the environmental, social and economic benefits related to the reduction of greenhouse gas emissions. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

It is estimated that with an annual recovery of 3% the ozone hole could close in 2060, net of the reduction of this progression caused by an increase in emissions from China. We certainly did not achieve this by banning spray canisters. I do not want to be disrespectful of this House, but I am taking this emblematic example to say that putting oneself in a confrontation between negationism and catastrophism, what is happening here again today, is worse than doing nothing. The responsibility that belongs to us as a Parliament, in the face of the emotional waves that cyclically follow on various issues over the decades, is precisely to look at the situation and find sustainable solutions. When it comes to sustainable development, it is a question of trying to reconcile the needs of the present generation with those of the future. It would therefore be easy to give in to this nonsense today, perhaps quoting my colleague Mr Rubbia who, from the top of an academic experience recognized worldwide, disagrees with so many things that have been supported. It might be controversial to say that the rain and the cold have arrived and that the May that we have faced, despite Greta's presence and calls in this branch of Parliament, has certainly been different from that of recent years. [[Applause from the L-SP-PSd'Az Group]] DE PETRIS (Misto-LEU). But the weather is confused with the climate: but what are we talking about? BRIZIERELLI (L-SP-PSd'Az). You could play this game, but as a League Group we don't want to lend ourselves and I just make one last call. We cannot, for example, go along with an emotional push that has led the country to give up nuclear energy and, at the same time, to close its eyes on the purchase from France of energy produced by nuclear power stations: more must be done. For this reason, in the motion that we have tabled, rather than focusing on the premises, we wanted to work firmly, side by side, with the Movimento 5 Stars on the disposition part, because we all need concrete facts and not merely declarations of intent. Therefore, in the nine points on which the Government is committed - and I will then begin to conclude - we wanted to offer declarations of principle, but we wanted to do so in such a way that our country does not start a technological-energy suicide to the detriment of the current population. The economy can and must be decarbonised, but it must still be done guaranteeing the security of the country's energy system. We can and must work towards sustainable mobility and we can and must move towards overall sustainability, but it must be done - as indicated, for example, in point 6.a) - by promoting infrastructure development policies and actions aimed at promoting virtuous initiatives of urban and non-urban sustainable mobility. This must be done because otherwise - let me be forced - we could safely proceed to the extinction of mankind, if not to the return to the Stone Age, and certainly we would impact less. My colleague, Fedeli, said that surely human actions and activity affect the planet; there would be no more! But then we have to find a way to guarantee our lifestyle by focusing on technological innovation and research and not looking at a happy decline. With the motion we tabled, we wanted to look in this direction. I conclude by saying that this device falls on World Environment Day but above all after a year of government activity which, in small and large measures, has tried to go in this direction and would suffice to give examples that compete with us. It is true that the climate does not stop at the borders, but we at national level have to look at these. When in the budget maneuver we have identified additional resources for the water network in Basilicata - and we all know what situations it is - we have invested resources on energy efficiency, we have given the municipalities the opportunity to act for the prevention of hydrogeological failure, we have acted and chosen to allocate resources in this direction. This is our responsibility: not simply declarations of principle but subsequent and responsible actions by subjects who must govern the country. We have done the same with the unblocking measure, which we shall be voting in a few hours' time, within which there have been courageous choices, the result of debate and confrontation. After a year a reasonable and shared solution was found by the League and Movimento 5 Star Groups on the end of waste problem. You know very well what the situation was regarding the blocking of plant authorisations; well, for the first time, after a comparison of a year, we have given an answer on this too. [[Applause from the L-SP-PSd'Az Group]] In fact, it is not simply up to us to point out problems but to find concrete, practicable and immediate solutions because the great principles are pursued by small actions. This is what we have to do; this is what we are doing and for this reason the Lega Group will vote in favour of its motion, which indicates a concrete path, and against the others that make an ideological battle only an opportunity for confrontation, without giving concrete solutions for our country. ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u67	GALLONE (FI-BP). Mr President, representatives of the Government, ladies and gentlemen, the traditional economy did not take into account, considering them unlimited, the cost of some essential materials: air, water, soil and land, elements from which we draw everything we need, or rather everything we need to live. Growing up and progressing over time we had to realize that the earth is not a boundless prairie but is perhaps more like a spaceship, launched into space with a crew of 7 billion people who must learn to preserve what is there, to defend the only environment of life and to recycle everything they can. In fact, if the supplies were finished, no one could send us a rescue shuttle. On climate change, for intellectual honesty, it is necessary to clarify the premise, precisely because we grew up with "Superquark": man has certainly helped to speed up a climate process, but it certainly derives also from obvious natural elements. The history of the earth tells us this: eras of desertification have always succeeded in ice age. This does not mean that in our day too often there is a lack of environmental respect, that there are not yet complete knowledge of the problem and awareness, that must be generated using every possible tool from those who govern and administer, with laws and regulations targeted and with support to every virtuous activity realized by the agencies and associations operating at every level to stop a process that seems unstoppable. As every year, today, June 5, is celebrated the World Environment Day promoted by the General Assembly of the United Nations, which this year dedicates the edition precisely to the issues of air pollution and the climate crisis. The slogan is beat air pollution: to defeat air pollution. The focus is on actions, even individual ones, to foster a community response and engage governments in the fight to save the planet. The official events will be hosted by China, a country with a very high rate of air pollution, but which begins to be - hopefully all - also a laboratory of initiatives for a green conversion. Which we hope because most of the problems arise precisely in Asia and, without criteria of reciprocity of the rules, also the processes of exchange and commercial collaboration would become problematic (the famous opening to the Silk Road). I am pleased that the House is also celebrating an issue that is becoming increasingly at the heart of the world debate, because it is precisely from the decision-makers and the legislator that every action of support and environmental respect must start (becoming Parliament as the first active and example, continuous stimulus for the action of those who govern), above all starting from forms of support and educational actions, passing through incentives in every form and measure for virtuous enterprises and in particular the application of every possible form of bureaucratic simplification, up to bilateral agreements. I was struck by what Susanna Tamaro wrote a few days ago about Sunday's Corriere, denouncing the bureaucratic interference that drives those who want to deal with this issue, for example, in agriculture. We will then draw inspiration from future policy actions because actions, the climate crisis, environmental protection and subsequent actions are choices that are necessary for human survival, but above all they represent an extraordinary opportunity to create jobs and generate a new sustainable economy. The choice and duty to support the so-called green economy, the green economy, is particularly good for the employment market. Since Forza Italia del lavoro does its battle, it will also fight on the green economy aimed at the employment market. In Italy there are already two million green jobs, of green jobs, which represent about 13% of the total national employment. I have chosen to be part of the Environment Commission because of the deep conviction that it has always, more than ever before, started from here, from nature and its elements. The Environment Commission, which was a little overwhelmed and undervalued at the time, should now become a pivot Commission around which everything should turn: economic and labour issues, health, tourism, agriculture, infrastructure, housing, growth and sustainable development, through education, education and culture. The figure of Greta Thunberg has aroused clamor and reactions worldwide, because it has touched above all the sensitivity of the younger ones. I, however, who are less young, found myself in front of a sort of déjà vu. Almost thirty years ago, in fact, on the occasion of the Earth Summit, held in Rio de Janeiro in 1992, another girl had already catalysed the attention of politics and the media; she was only twelve years old and went on stage as a representative of a delegation of boys who fought for the environment. His name was Severn Cullis - Suzuki, but most remember her as the little girl who silenced the world for six minutes, giving an incredible speech in front of the United Nations, in which he claimed, for his and future generations, the inalienable right to life. He emphasized, among other things, the dramatic effects of human action on the environment and climate change. Did it really take two young girls, thirty years later, to engage in a process of awareness on environmental and climate issues worldwide? Do we adult decision makers, in possession of all the tools to promote a change in the tendency to preserve natural resources, not succeed in putting in place a coordinated and synergistic action? This House is proof of this today, with a series of motions of which only one was adopted and the others were not considered. Today I would have expected everyone in this House to look in the same direction. [[Applause from the FI-BP Group]] in this House It is not a personal or party problem, but it is a problem for everyone, for every single citizen and, above all, it is a problem for our children. The climate challenge, as a constraint, must be transformed into opportunities for economic growth, promoting a new economically sustainable development. This is why reversing the trend is important, for example by paying attention to a new environmental tax, as an imperative for future economic policies. Only in this way can Italy fully fit into the European process, moving taxation from work to pollution of production processes and products, after and during their use. The change must start urgently and encourage tax systems that benefit the use of renewable and sustainable environmental resources, and in this perspective Italy can play a leading role, including at European level. Attention: accelerating the transition does not mean slowing down the infrastructural and technological process, as many would like, but rather a dynamic commitment, aimed at conceiving investments in large works, such as the high-speed train, the modernization of the railway network, new state-of-the-art transport systems (more competitive, efficient and cost-effective, from the point of view of the environmental impact, of the road transport, especially with regard to the goods). We come to the issue of waste, another very important issue for the environment and the climate. Italy is the European country with the highest percentage of recycling on all waste (urban, industrial and mining) and this also thanks to innovative methods of waste management and advanced systems for their recovery. ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u70	MORONESE (M5S). Mr President, ladies and gentlemen, the contrast to the dramatic consequences of climate change requires strong and incisive action, which takes into account in a holistic way all the actions that can be implemented in order to have an effective impact on the prevention and gradual cancellation of all those emissive sources, which are literally stifling our planet. It is time to act and act quickly, because what we have called development in the last two centuries has irreparably undermined the natural balances of the previous millennia and threatens today the hopes of the new generations and those who will come after them. Climate change is already in place, it is nature that rebels, it is a chain phenomenon that affects all the regions of the world, whose catastrophic consequences caused by extreme weather phenomena are bringing to its knees vast areas of the world and reaping victims among the populations, including Italy. We know that we need a choral and global commitment because the commitment of a single nation is not enough. In fact, even when it seems that all states and all communities around the world agree to adopt policies to combat climate change, there are rumors of distinction, distanced, sometimes genuine rethinking and denials on the one hand and on the other of the oceans. As long as it is a matter of stating good intentions, perhaps to be advertised on electoral occasions or agitated in the presence of an opponent, everyone can find words and images capable of fascinating and even convincing. In so doing, one can also attribute a Nobel to the IPCC scientists, admire Greta's tenacity, convene a solemn world conference a year or ask for the state of environmental and climate emergency in the country, as done by the UK and recently fumigated in our home by the Democratic Party and also by Senator De Petris in their motions (which only serves to leverage mediatically). 11:52 a.m. (Moronese follow-up). From speeches to actions, from words to actions. We want to avoid seeing that carnival of contradictions that, in essence, serves to keep things as they are. The planetary question is still of supremacy, domination and division of roles between North and South of the planet, between East and West, between mature economies up to opulence and increasingly aggressive emerging economies. The confrontation of the moment, that on duties, is nothing more than a chapter of the same comedy and in too many respects also the policies of safeguarding the global environment are played and exchanged on the same table of a game that, sometimes, tastes to be spotlessly rigged. China, the world's first economic power, does not believe that it must commit itself to saving fossil fuels, reducing emissions into the atmosphere and correcting its development model to be redirected in a more respectful sense to natural balances. Yet, that economy, together with that of the European continent, is the most responsible for the climatic changes of the last two centuries. If the model of success - the one that established the primacy of the West and guaranteed well-being to almost a billion people - continues to be the one that broke the natural balances, depleted and squandered the primary resources, polluted, deserted and anthropized increasing portions of land, how will it be possible to deny the same benefits to those peoples of the developing economies, which that same model adopted and want to pursue? They will certainly not be solemn environmentalist paternals to dissuade those peoples from seeking their own well-being. Life on the planet - globalization or not - is irreparably interdependent. It will not be the Hungarian wall that will prevent acid rain from falling on Budapest and it will not be the Mexican wall that will prevent the rising oceans on the American coasts. Just as the West has baptized and nourished that old model of development, of which we now live all the harmful consequences, it is now up to the same to inaugurate a new beginning, to create confidence in a different development and to offer the hope of well-being on a planet that will have a future for humanity and all living species. The Kyoto Protocol, the father of all the agreements for the protection of the planetary ecosystem, is almost thirty years old. It just doesn't seem to have succeeded in intent, if we look at the current alarms and the state of the planet. However, there is no alternative to an unceasing, daily and determined commitment to persuasion, conviction and accountability. There is no alternative to having to decide, now and here, which measures to take, which actions to trigger, which projects and what strategies to implement. Inertia would be an unforgivable fault. Shortcuts and cunning may override the most approximate consciousness at the moment, but they would soon reveal their fragility. To declare the state of climate emergency, as the United Kingdom has done, postponing to 2050 the achievement of tangible results of mitigation of the devastating effects produced by the production system of that country, looks much like a vicious promise from sailor. It would be very encouraging to learn instead that, for example, the British Government has banned the export of municipal waste for millions of tons and for almost ten years they have been going through the North Sea to be incinerated in all European countries. If the United Kingdom really wanted to be a pioneer in the circular economy, it would really be a great opportunity to demonstrate leadership in the management of its urban waste. I would also like to point out that we Italians are well aware of the state of emergency, especially for the damage that has left us the countless states of emergency proclaimed after earthquakes, floods, fires, droughts and also for waste management. Each of them has left us an account of billions of euros spent in derogation from ordinary laws, with extraordinary measures often oblique and ineffective, with lobbying and cliques that have grazed beautifully on the ruins (in fact remained untouched at the end of the emergency state). This is not the way, this is not the way and this is no longer the time of extraordinaryness: it is the time of stubborn, stubborn and tenacious daily mobilization to really change things in concrete and deep. This is why the motion under discussion today, presented by the Movimento 5 Stars and the entire majority group, formally wants to commit the Government to put in place serious and concrete actions aimed at decarbonising the economy, setting as its objective the strategy for a modern and climate neutral economy, contained in the Communication of the European Commission COM (2018) 773 of 28 November 2018. On decarbonisation yesterday, the Minister for the Environment, Sergio Costa, to whom there is no doubt to be recognized a serious, concrete and incisive action, signed with the European Commission, the Italian Municipalities and Regions the Action Plan for the improvement of air quality, which includes concrete actions in the field of rationalization of environmentally harmful subsidies, reduction of emissions from biomass, city micromobility, limitations for polluting heating systems and diesel oil and much more. All this will be done by involving the territorial authorities and will be invested 400 million euros per year. The municipalities will therefore also have the economic cover to carry out these actions. Mr President, the motion responds firmly and pragmatically to the call of the European Commission, which commits all Member States to a profound economic, energy and social transformation, in order to effectively meet the temperature reduction objectives agreed at the Paris Climate Conference COP21 in December 2015. It also calls on national parliaments to identify the strategic components for achieving the objective of a climate-neutral Europe by 2050. It will not be a motion to improve the quality of our lives tomorrow morning, but it is certain that the one we are debating, anchored in shared objectives in Europe and in international fora, is pressing and promotes in a precise and timely manner the adoption of binding standards and actions, which tomorrow morning could begin to improve the quality of our daily lives. In conclusion, Mr President, perhaps today we entrust one of the hardest tasks of the legislature to this Government, which we are sure will want to respect, because this is not about partisan political issues but about the life of our children and the future of the next generations. I therefore declare on behalf of the Movimento 5 Stars the vote in favour of our motion at first signature L'Abbate and against all others. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u86	MARGIOTTA (PD). Mr President, we are at a time when the communication prevails over the truth, sometimes also leading to errors and incidents, such as those we witnessed earlier, when we unanimously rejected the amendment by Senator Coltorti, which had to be withdrawn, but was not in the right time, or at a moment of confusion by the good rapporteur on an opinion on text 2 of Amendment 1.7 by Senator Pergreffi, because the communication gave way to the idea that the amendment by Senator Pergreffi suspended the procurement code. That wasn't true. He suspended five aspects of the procurement code. Even in the case of amendment 1.7 (text 2)/161 (text 2) it is said that it suspends the procurement code, whereas it is not true, because it suspends only three of the five aspects; moreover, it suspends them by returning to the text of the original decree and the two which are theoretically suspended are rewritten exactly as they were in the original text of the decree. In short, so much noise for nothing; it happened only that we returned to the starting point, there is no suspension and we continue to work in an absolutely normal way, except that everyone will sell the matter as he wants in the communication. The League will say that they have suspended the code and that is not true; the Movimento 5 Stars will say that it is not true that they have suspended it, that they have kept the point and, as I will explain, that is not true either. The amendment we are talking about, which is certainly better than that of Senator Pergreffi, continues to contain some aberrant forecasts. The three points in which it affects are the following: no reduction and no qualification of the number of contracting stations, no appeal for the Selection Committees to external commissioners selected by the DAC (each one can appoint whom he wants) and, above all, the total restoration of the integrated contract, for which Senator Cioffi looks at me perplexed, but of which I will speak during the examination of the next amendment. I now want to talk about the first two issues: one has the illusion that the code has blocked the works, but the numbers say that it is not true, so much so that the Economic and Social Research Centre of the Building Market (Cresme) claims that last year more jobs were contracted than in the past. What is true is that the most important thing, the most serious vulnus in the sector, is that of the qualification and reduction of contracting stations. Invitalia, which is a good contracting station, did 221 races in force code, with only 7 percent of appeals and litigation. Anas and RFI continued to race and contract jobs. It is the lack of quality and qualification of small contracting stations that blocks the system, and what do you do? It even abhors the mandatory use of purchasing power stations, returning to virtually 35,000 procurement stations in the country. But this is not enough for you, do something else, this yes, aberrant: you think that the jury can be cooked at home. In other words, a mayor or sole head of the procedure (RUP) can choose exactly the commissioners he wants, he does not have the obligation to resort to the DAC's register, nor to ask for the requirements of professionalism and morality, but he can choose who he wants: his brother, his cousin, his sister's boyfriend or something like that. The combined arrangement of the increase of the contracting stations and the possibility to choose the Selection Committees as it is intended to determine those that authoritative protagonists of the field have defined criminal norms. Don't be offended, but that's what it's about. You don't understand who benefits, but this is what you do with your amending proposal: go back to a system that will cause so many problems in the country. I will talk about the other negative aspects when we discuss Senator Pergreffi's amendment 1.7 (text2). [[Applause from the PD Group.]]
ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u87	I ask you to speak for an explanation of vote.
ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u88	You have the right to do so.
ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u89	DE PETRIS (Misto-LEU). Mr President, I do not know whether we are faced with a game of the parties, an art comedy or the dramatization of statements, which announce one thing and then another. It is in fact, Mr President, that the amendment under consideration, at the first signature of Senator Patuanelli, makes some changes to the text that came out of the Commission, in particular remains 30% with regard to the evaluation of the most economically advantageous offer, but basically so much thunder, that it did not rain: in the end, somehow, we have returned to that, but the declarations of suspension of the code and everything else remain. Obviously this does not mean that we are giving a positive assessment; indeed, all the absolutely and fiercely critical issues remain, which we have expressed in relation to the text that came out of the Commission. It is certainly different from the amendment by Senator Pergreffi, who proposed the total liberalization of subcontracting (perhaps Salvini, when he talks about respect for the European directive, speaks only and only about the case of the procedure). In fact, however, the text under consideration returns to a sort of 40% mediation, which we still consider absolutely serious, because the aspect of subcontracting, as well as the fact that we no longer draw on external commissioners and the fact that we return to the multiplication of contracting stations, without any work on qualification, will ensure that, in our system, these rules risk being criminal: I call them for what they are. It's no use for you to keep pretending nothing. All these rules will make the entire procurement system permeable to very heavy infiltrations. Do not tell me that the measure will unlock the yards; we will talk about it in some time, when we will find ourselves - once again - in a situation of total blockade. There is another serious element, already contained in Senator Pergreffi's amendment. The amendment signed by Senator Patuanelli also rewrites paragraph 24 on waste. Honestly this rule has improved compared to paragraph 24, but - I say this to Senator Patuanelli - there is one thing you have to explain to me. First of all, you should have ensured, once and for all, that the Ministry of the Environment and the Protection of the Territory and the Sea adopt the ministerial decrees and, above all, to make a serious operation, you should have provided not only that all the authorizations issued by the various authorities and the Regions should be sent to the Ministry of the Environment and the protection of the territory and the sea, but also that the latter can intervene and correct in order to ensure that in our country there is a homogeneous application of the rules on waste and not linked to the different regions and authorities that issue the authorizations. Having said that, of course, our vote can only be against this other major amendment. [[Applause from the Mixed Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u123	DE BERTOLDI (FdI). Mr President, this is one of the classic amendments that I would like to make in common sense, on which I would like to see a cross-cutting sharing, as it is essentially intended to make life easier for businesses and those civil servants and civil servants working in the procurement field. We know that the administration, in certain situations and with certain suppliers, has the right to suspend the obligation of the security, even with gains in terms of price. With this amendment, we propose that by law it should be established that small or very small securities (i.e. up to EUR 500) are not due. Basically, we would like to prevent the municipalities, the provinces and, in general, the public officials from going crazy in producing securities documents up to 500 euros (i.e. perhaps equal to 100, 200 or 300 euros). I believe that providing that securities up to EUR 500 are not really due is a common sense rule, which meets the needs of both civil servants and those companies which, for ridiculous figures, have absurd administrative costs and burdens. I therefore appeal to all my fellow Members to accept a common sense rule, which involves a slight variation in order to achieve greater simplification.

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u195	Mr President, the decree-law before us should probably have dealt only with reconstruction following seismic events, since two very important draft laws are already under discussion in Parliament: the first, on the revision of the procurement code, which would have avoided fragmentation and partial resumption of the code; the second, on urban regeneration, which has been under discussion in the Environment and Agriculture Commissions for nine months. However, I have tabled amendments to Article 5 because they told me that, since the article on urban regeneration is now there, we should to some extent contain it. This is because of a problem due to a decision of the TAR, more than ten years ago, on which the Council of State expressed itself in September 2017. In relation to Ministerial Decree No 1444 of 1968 - which the constant jurisprudence considers a reference of law not derogable to guarantee collective needs related to hygiene and safety - it is specified what was the concern of many, that in historical centers, whether consolidated or not, the killing and reconstruction, therefore a fundamental action of urban regeneration, could entail the need to maintain the distance of 10 meters, as provided for in Article 9, paragraphs 1 and 2 of the same decree. The Council of State had already pronounced itself in the judgment of September 2017 saying that, with regard to the decree in question, Article 9 refers only to the new planning of the territory, and therefore to new buildings, not to demolitions and reconstructions, and specifies that when it comes to abatement and reconstruction it is not really recommendable to evaluate a different distance from the existing one, because the withdrawal of the silhouette of the building could involve other types of problems, such as the closure of interstitial spaces or in any case the invasion of other property. Therefore, in the amendments to which I am a signatory I have pedestically reported what the Council of State specifies in the judgment because, although the judgments clearly make jurisprudence, it is probably right that the legislators place a point of reflection, which however does not constitute a derogation, as has been attempted with other amendments, aimed at limiting the operation of Article 9 only to particular areas (zonations). Wanting to take advantage, however, of this article, which refers to urban regeneration, I also wanted to make a reference to an existing law. I repeat that it has been nine months - with hundreds of hearings and twelve draft laws being examined by the Agriculture and Environment Committees - that we are discussing an important bill and a general reorder text, since we already have some confusion with over twenty regional laws. Law No 10 of 14 January 2013 provides for rules for the development of urban green spaces. Paragraph 2 of Article 6 states that: For the purposes of soil saving and the preservation of non-urbanised municipal areas, the municipalities may... and list four points which may be used to provide for special measures of advantage aimed at promoting the reuse and reorganization of residential settlements and to provide appropriate tools for intervention on existing assets and green areas. In my amendment 5.15 I simply replaced'may' with'must', given the urgency that indiscriminate land use is causing our territory, even in terms of altered climate and increased pollution. Wanting to take advantage again, I added another paragraph b-bis) relating to the database of the existing unused real estate and disused areas, which is primary and no longer derogable to continue to build on the built. The amendments in question are three and I call on the House, which has debated climate change motions this morning, to reflect on the fact that derogating from Ministerial Decree No 1444 of 1968 means derogating from constitutional rights, just as narrative has taught us for over fifty years. PRESIDENT. - The debate is The remaining amendments are set out. I invite the rapporteurs and the representative of the Government to comment on these amendments.

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u201	MARTELLI (Mixed). Mr President, Amendment 5.1 is deletion of the article for reasons which have been well expressed by Mrs Nugnes: an article of this kind, for which there is no evidence of necessity or urgency, should not have been included in a decree-law. Not only that; what does article 5 of dangerous, in addition to all the things that have already been announced? Simply go to the first paragraph, letter a) that, speaking of the faculty of the Regions to introduce specific norms in the field of urban planning, affirms that the words "can preview" of the current legislation are replaced by the following: "introduce." This gives the regions the right to introduce legislation that goes beyond national legislation on land use and regeneration and urban planning. In this regard, it should first of all be noted that, as far as land consumption is concerned, this is in fact consistent with one of the proposals that were included in the commitments of the majority motion, in which we were talking about measures for responsible land use, which is to say play with moderation, or to drink as little as possible. Considering that there are twelve bills that talk about zeroing and stopping land consumption, hearing about reasonable land consumption reminds me of gambling advertising. That is the first point. The second is that there is not yet a shared notion of urban regeneration. There are lines that point to the lighthouse talking about urban regeneration and regeneration of the urbanistic fabric intended as built up and there is a strand that instead speaks of regeneration as a vision of regeneration from the point of view of the human being, which very much reminds the division that is at this time relative to the restoration: philological, the Violett-le-Duc and restoration not restoration. At a time when there is no clear definition on which to lean to talk about urban regeneration, the most dangerous thing you can do is to put your hand in it and empower the regions to legislate separately on the matter. These two reasons, combined, should suggest a minimum of prudence because we cannot reason with the idea that the Regions are all virtuous. The principle is: I don't want to know what the good spirit of the law is, but to understand what negative things a law would allow you to do. With all the good will of the world and given what has happened at levels of national urbanism, such legislation is like getting all the oxen out and then going to look for them at night. We believe that this is a problem which can become even more important, and that is why Article 5 should be deleted. [[Applause from Senator Nugnes.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u205	Mr Ferrazzi (PD). Mr President, I have already had the opportunity to explain in detail in the illustration of the minority report how Article 5, which should relaunch our country on urban regeneration, is quite empty. The title and the first paragraph of Article 5 have remained, which explains what the article should have done; after that the nullity of nothingness, because there is no link between the title and the article. So, Mr President, with Amendment 5.4 we do one very simple thing: we fill the article with content. And we do so with an overall vision, taking into account two fundamental prerequisites for regeneration: balance and environmental sustainability; balance and economic sustainability. Those who lose sight of one of these two elements speak vainly because in the countries where regeneration is done there is attention to regeneration from an environmental, cultural and social point of view and there is the incentive to do so. In fact, regeneration is done with public funds, but it is mainly done through private investments. Our amendment goes in exactly this direction: a great deal of attention to the environment, culture, rehabilitation and urban security, while creating the conditions for these interventions to be truly possible. In short, there is a very clear definition of what we mean by urban regeneration, of what must be the resources and competences, between the state, regions and local authorities. It defines a fundamental concept, which is that of public interest - the territories or areas of cities that are defined as of interest for urban regeneration are of public interest - and there is a declaration of public utility and consequently it can also intervene according to the code of expropriations. This applies to individual buildings, building complexes and entire blocks and it is applicable for building renovation, energy redevelopment, demolition - i.e. what the urban regulations currently call building replacement, i.e. felling and reconstruction - and earthquake-proof adaptation. There is, of course, a strong focus on protecting cultural heritage and historic centers. There is the whole question of the centrality of institutional investors and therefore pension funds, professional funds and real estate funds. Then there is the remodulation of Ministerial Decree No. 1444 of 1968, because in our country we have the paradox of a framework law of urbanism of 1942, whose implementing legislation is of 1968. We must put our hands back on it, because the expansionary urban planning of those years must no longer have anything to do with today's regenerative urban planning. Let me give you an example: we have to adjust construction costs and urbanization costs, because it is not the same thing to build a shit, in energy class G, or redevelopment, with energy class A. Construction costs should be adjusted until felling. With this amendment there is also a great deal of attention to the quality of design in public competitions. Finally, there is all the attention on tax incentives and in particular on Tasi, Tari, reduction of up to 50% of taxes and fees of any kind, decrease of the contribution for release to build, mortgage registration tax and fixed cadastral. Obviously we are always talking about the areas that the municipality defines as urban regeneration and that therefore the City Council, votes for the good of the city. Moreover, the deduction of 65% is expected, stable, for the renovation of the building, the energy and anti-seismic redevelopment and the demolition and a deduction of 50% of the gross tax relating to the amount paid for the purchase of a real estate unit in class B and, last, incentives for the interventions of energy retrofit. Mr President, I should like to end by saying that we also include the concept of administrative continuity, i.e. once the public interest has been defined, the next administration cannot cancel the regeneration programming, but must demonstrate, in doing so, that there is a prevailing public interest. [[Applause from the PD Group]] We ask all the senators to vote on the amendment, because this amendment is a regeneration and without this amendment they are chatting. [[Applause from the PD Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u273	GIAMMANCO (FI-BP). Mr President, we are about to vote on an amendment that I have signed with my colleagues in the Committee on Constitutional Affairs, which provides for EUR 160 million for the installation of video surveillance systems in kindergartens, nursery schools, facilities for the elderly and disabled. I want to thank the majority who supported it, because at last what I urge from 2009 with a bill of mine seems to be possible. For ten years now, in fact, I have proposed this protection measure in favour not only of the weakest subjects (children, elderly and disabled), but also of the operators and teachers who daily work with great self-denial and great professionalism within these structures (nurseries, nursery schools and structures for the elderly and disabled). Of course, in order to determine the timing and the way in which this measure is to be implemented, the adoption of the draft law on video surveillance, which I am rapporteur for and which is currently under discussion in the Committee on Constitutional Affairs, will be absolutely essential and necessary. I hope, therefore, that no more time will be wasted and that, after the adoption of this amendment, the next step is precisely to approve this draft law which does not include - I would like to remind you - only video surveillance in the places where the most fragile and vulnerable people live, but also much more, various measures to prevent and combat the phenomenon of ill-treatment to children, the elderly and the disabled, such as those concerning training. It is now a social emergency, with which we deal every day. This is yet another news of abuse that would take place in a school of the children of the Avellinese, where four teachers were arrested, accused of repeated abuse of their pupils. Thanks to hidden cameras installed in January, we saw what actually happened in these classrooms. A lot of time could have been saved if the cameras had already been installed, but a long time has passed and it has emerged that these arrested teachers exercised physical and psychological harassment on the children they should have taken care of. One of these teachers is even accused of sexual violence. "Shut up, don't cry, I have to hang you": this is just one of the many phrases spoken against these small victims by the teachers and surely these small victims will need psychological support because they will remain marked for life by this experience. I believe that we can no longer allow reports like this to continue to follow. As legislators, it is our duty to give concrete answers, effective answers that can really put an end to this sad phenomenon and to do this, it is necessary for this House to approve as soon as possible the draft law that I have been proposing for several years. This amendment will have to be the first step towards this next common objective, if we do not want this amendment that we are going to vote on to remain a dead letter and it is just a mirror for the lark, to attract consensus. We move forward, therefore, we are in the right direction, but soon we will come to approve my draft law on the subject which will give an answer to the many families who ask us to do so. [[Applause from the FI-BP and M5S groups.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u279	SAPONARA (L-SP-PSd'Az). Mr President, this is a very important amendment that we are about to vote on. It is the basis for the conclusion of a draft law which has been before the Committee on Constitutional Affairs for some months now. We have auditioned several subjects and I really feel like supporting Senator Giammanco, because this bill must be brought to an end. With the establishment of this fund, important foundations certainly are laid. To briefly retrace the process of this amendment, it started with the Committee on Constitutional Affairs as a proposal from the Lega Group, signed by the undersigned and shared and signed by the other members of the Commission belonging to the other political forces. There was, therefore, a full sharing of this amendment, which then saw improvements and adjustments in its path, in order to finally receive a very favourable opinion in the joint committees which examined it. I therefore hope that this amendment will be voted on by a majority and that the draft law can really be concluded. We are waiting for the opinion of the MEF, which automatically obliges us to wait further time to complete the examination of the bill, which more than ever becomes necessary. I am also saying this in the light of the fact that in recent days I have received several phone calls from various administrators who ask when it can be implemented because they are already ready in the municipalities to implement it. Amendment 19.0.1 (text 3) is certainly an important basis from which I hope to conclude the examination of the draft law on video surveillance. [[Applause from the L-SP-PSd'Az Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u282	MALPEZZI (PD). Mr President, I would just like to clarify a little. Even when the rapporteur presented the overall measure, she said that at last in Article 19 we have the amendment that inserts video surveillance in schools. In fact, amendment 19.0.1 (text 3) inserts a fund, and in fact paragraph 3 tells us that this fund must be regulated by a particular law, that is, the one that was illustrated earlier. This is because in reality the bill - which is at second reading here in the Senate, held in the first Commission - had already been approved in the House, but without resources. What did that mean? That the costs of the measure, as arrived here by us, would have fallen on the shoulders of the schools, in particular of the equal schools, since there was obligatoryness, and of the local authorities. The above amendment creates the fund to prevent local authorities and equal schools from finding themselves in difficulty in the face of compulsory schooling, because they cannot have autonomous resources to provide for them. In any case, the bill is much more complex, fortunately, and it cannot be limited only and exclusively to the video surveillance that, I repeat, is present here because a guarantee fund is foreseen to be able to bring home the provision, but that is built for everything an article - this is very important - on training. Colleagues, prevention - and we are convinced of this - is done first of all by investing in training, measures for our teachers, by ensuring that burnout syndrome is prevented as much as possible and by always protecting the educating community. In this regard, I have to say that, with the serious work done by the 1st Commission, and thanks to a comprehensive review of the text as serious by the rapporteur, we have succeeded. Therefore, this cannot be used only and exclusively as an electoral spot by liquidating it with a... we put video surveillance in schools... because it would not reflect the spirit of that norm - to which this same amendment refers to paragraph 3 - which, I repeat, sees an in-depth work instead. Finally, we have the courage to say that prevention is first and foremost by investing in training, in the preparation of workers, in the fact that accreditations for structures are reviewed. Often, in fact, we have verified, even during these years, that some episodes of violence against the elderly and the disabled have occurred because those structures could not stand there and the forms of accreditation were not valid. There is therefore a seriousness of work and, given the cooperation with which the measure was dealt with in the first Commission, I was very sorry when it was dismissed as a pure electoral slogan, as a sort of 'we finally have video surveillance in schools'. It doesn't work that way, colleagues. The moment you need a camera means that someone has already suffered abuse, violence. In order to prevent this, we must start from something else, and I repeat, thanks to the serious work done in the first Commission today, we have a text which takes into account in a strong way the whole of the part relating to workers, their training and preparation. I say it at Mom's: I don't want my daughters to go to a school just because there are cameras. I want them to go quietly to school because they know they have trained and valid teachers.
