You have postponed for weeks, because you are interested only in the election campaign, in insulting yourself from morning to night, on social media or on TV, and in playing theater. You've wasted time and days: for you one day it's nothing, but for a business or for a worker waiting, one day it can be everything. This is what our amendments talk about, how to support instruments to create and defend jobs and services, since you do not add a single euro to what we have allocated in the last parliamentary term. These are amendments on how to find the reasons to remain in our territories, to repopulate them, to defend vital services such as education. We ask that the intervention for the schools of the small municipalities of the crater be confirmed, so that they do not lose teachers and that they do not close. This is the need of children, families, those who live there where everything is more difficult, where a multi-class does not only mean shared identity, but means a place where to design their own future. During all these days, the mayors, with the tricolor bands, have never stopped protesting, while you were doing yet another scene and have never stopped making their voices heard, to try to rekindle those spotlights, which in all these months you have turned off. One mayor told me that for them the earthquake doesn't even deserve the title of a law. He's right, you can't deny it. We have asked you for an ad hoc measure, specifically on Central Italy, which should be the largest construction site in Europe, but which you have kept paralyzed for a year because of indifference and presumption. You have disgraced our laws, but you know perfectly well that the decrees adopted have allowed to protect income and moral estate, because no one has been left alone and no one has been left behind, billions have been invested and - for the first time - a total coverage has been provided for the second houses as well. Above all, reconstruction has been linked to a broader social economic project to think about the role of Apennine areas for the coming decades. For your part, however, there is no idea, no strategy, and even today's measure is absolutely inadequate. Undersecretary Crimi, I see you're busy on the phone, but where's the shock decree you bragged of? This cannot be the case, because you have ignored almost all the requests of the Regions voted at the unified conference, despite the fact that Minister Toninelli was absent twice in two. We needed micro technical measures to speed up a regulatory system that works; these are measures without charges, but you did not include them in the measure. In the same way, there are no tools to relocate production activities, nor the possibility of using expenditure savings to continue work contracts in the special offices for reconstruction and in the municipalities. For a year we have been asking you for an intervention to address critical issues that in 2016 we could not know, but you have not done so and you have transformed the reconstruction into a further emergency. It is true, Mr President, that there were excessive rigidities in our approach, but they were dictated by a need that has a strong political value, namely to avoid criminal infiltration and to stop illegal attempts which, in other cases, have blocked the work and done enormous damage. We say woe to lower our guard even today, because it is the keeping of our communities. That is why we have put a question to the President of the Council of Ministers on the desirability of more transparent rules and on the very important donations of solidarity and charity of private individuals, also in the light of the judicial investigations concerning the Municipality of Visso. Mr President, gentlemen of the government, try talking to the mayors of the crater: they will tell you that they have been abandoned in the last year and that this climate of detachment between municipalities and government creates humiliation and frustration among technicians, staff and citizens, who should instead be moved to react. They will tell you that talking to Commissioner Farabollini is like talking empty with a rubber wall. Some of the things that we have been asking for for for months, which we are late in today, and we support them today, and indeed we also claim them to be our merit. I am thinking of the recruitment of staff, the extension of the heavy pay envelope, the direct choice in private reconstruction for small jobs, which is very important, but which we are asking of you in compliance with the transparency rules of the procurement code. However, much, much is still missing. That is why we ask you to approve our amendments, in order to equate productive construction with housing, to give more role to professionals and to protect workers with the obligation to apply national collective agreements, and to support small crater enterprises to access the work of reconstruction. Above all - and I conclude - we ask you to do this in order to ensure that the question of the internal areas of our Apennines returns to the centre. Colleagues, let us all do it together, again, a great and indispensable national issue. [[Applause from the PD Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u335	CRIMI, State Secretary for the Presidency of the Council of Ministers. Mr President, I am going to read it. It remains the same as "In order to avoid abandonment" phenomena up to "it is allowed"; then it continues directly with the words "the installation of temporary and removable structures, on the ground where the same buildings or on other land of property located in the territory of the same Municipality with any urban destination or on land also not owned or on other land on which there is a real right of enjoyment" with the addition of the words "previa acquisition of the declaration of availability by the property". It then goes on identical to be declared suitable for this purpose by a special municipal act until the end. Finally, the following is added: Within ninety days of the issuing of the order for the serviceability of the destroyed or damaged property, the persons referred to in the first period shall dismantle or remove the removable temporary structures referred to in this Article and restore the status of the places in question. The time constraint is thus more explicit: within ninety days of the declaration of agibility the status of the places must be restored and it is obviously borne by the property, therefore without any burden for the public finance. ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u348	PESCO (M5S). Mr President, during the investigation and analysis in the Commission, we made a request in relation to the part relating to land not owned by the applicant for the installation of removable structures. That part would have to be eliminated. I'm asking if she has to be left. Undersecretary Crimi, would you like to speak?
ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u349	Mr President, I respond to President Pesco. The ratio of removing that part was aimed - but I would like to understand if the interpretation is correct - at preventing it from becoming a general authorization to install temporary structures on other people's land. Therefore, in order to overcome this danger we have introduced in the proposed rewording the wording "previa acquisition of the availability of the property", as it is a temporary structure to be realized in totally destroyed areas where there are perimeter areas (the famous red areas) in which it is not easy that those who have had the destroyed house have the availability of a land, while it is possible, for usufruct rather than for convenient use, rent or in ways that can be agreed, to obtain the land of others, of which one has a right of enjoyment obviously subordinate to the acquisition of the availability, as is done in any activity that is exercised on another's land. That is not a general authorization; it is the urban authorization by the municipality that provides for this rule, except that the property authorizes the installation of a temporary structure in its own land. It is a very important norm for the areas that today have seen a high devastation, such as Amatrice, Accumoli, Arquata, and we could list many others.

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u374	Mr Pinotti (PD). Mr President, I am very sorry to have received the opposite opinion on these amendments, which relate to the collapse of the Morandi bridge and to the needs of the city of Genoa in particular, one of which is also related to the port of Savona, which also had problems, following all the inconveniences that there have been. These are very specific amendments, which have been studied precisely on the problems raised at the moment by citizenship, businesses or traders. They were therefore minimal demands, such as the small extension of the fund that is already established in the decree-law, because in reality there are many inconveniences also with regard to the explosions that are taking place to destroy the bridge. There was an amendment calling for 5 million deposits and loans to be included in the Guarantee Fund for micro and small enterprises, which have suffered greatly. There is also an amendment which calls for the possibility of reimbursement of the decrease in turnover to be postponed to 30 April - and not to the entry into force of the decree-law. Why such a date? Only that day, in fact, the road that had been blocked in the area of Campasso, under the Morandi bridge, for the risks associated with any further collapses, created the need for everyone to make very long routes. Also on this there was a small request for refreshment for all those who, having to lengthen the journey much, have spent more (so not only for the haulers who were already foreseen in the decree-law previous). These are therefore punctual needs and certainly not ideological flags, studied on real situations and problems that the city of Genoa is experiencing and also the economic demand was very contained. I am therefore here to represent a regret that the citizens of Genoa will continue to experience this discomfort. [[Applause from the PD Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u433	MARGIOTTA (PD). Mr President, I had announced that I would speak on Amendment 4,27. I find it absolutely serious that Article 4 (3) states first of all that extraordinary Commissioners may be entitled to assume the functions of contracting station directly. This, in my opinion, is already wrong, because the extraordinary Commissioners should be those who send the works, but not necessarily doing the contracts themselves. Moreover, in the paragraph it is written that they can contract the works in derogation from the laws in force: this frankly seems to me an extreme forcing. Let's be clear, that's what happened for Genoa, but that was an absolute emergency and in the end we all shared that we couldn't wait for the normal times of a race procedure. It cannot be thought that this institute can be used indiscriminately, because, I repeat, the provision does not specify the works on which the institute is to apply. If it is said that the commissioner can in fact assign the work to whom he wants, because this means to operate in derogation from the laws on public contracts, we do an enormity other than criminal, an enormity of which I fear we will all have to repent as a country system, but also falling into a clear contradiction. In fact, if the changes you are making to the code with the measure unblocks yards to our examination are positive, they work and streamline the procedures, why is it necessary that the Commissioner can operate by way of derogation from the same laws that you now say are good, effective and efficient? You make a law and then, at the same time, you say that the Commissioner can go on without using it and without applying it, by way of derogation, in fact choosing intuitu personae, the company that has to do the work. This, in theory, out of 100, 1,000, 2,000 or 3,000 works, because there is neither a roof nor a strict criterion, and without any clarification of how to operate. Frankly, when I first spoke about this in the Commission, I was convinced that in the face of these arguments they all agreed. It is so obvious that this is a devastating twist - I use this term once again - and I expected wisdom from the Government and the rapporteurs. I cannot understand how we can move forward on a matter that can have disastrous effects on our system, on transparency, on honesty and, in the end, on the same quality of the works that are being carried out. I know it is not time for second thoughts, but I really needed to leave these statements to the record, because otherwise it seems that everything goes into cavalry and that we do not realize what we are voting on. And here I can only tell you that when this Pesco said that there was a contrary opinion on amendment 4.13, he had thought: live God! This is because Amendment No 4.13 says that the approval of a project by a Commissioner exceeds anything else that prevents and authorizes, i.e. the archaeological, environmental or anything else. ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u437	Grasso (Misto-Leu). Mr President, I can only fully agree with Senator Margiotta's speech. We are now in the end and we have understood that this measure, which is about to become a law, has very little to do with the stated aim of unblocking the yards. It seems more generally to be about loosening the rules of transparency and supervision, underestimating the rigour necessary for authorisations, thus reducing the protection of cultural, environmental and landscape goods, and finally a systematic and unjustified reduction of the role and functions of the National Anti-corruption Authority. This return to the old regime, prior to the procurement code, is supplemented by the exhumation of the extraordinary commissioners. For Christ's sake, there will also be cases in which this is necessary and we have seen it in the case of Genoa. However, given the extent of the powers of derogation granted to Commissioners, it would be at least appropriate, with this amendment, which I consider to be of common sense, to provide for the obligation to justify the derogations, in such a way as to allow for possible judicial review by the administrative judiciary, from the point of view of the justification for those measures. You think that, as has already been said, Article 4 (3) assigns the functions of contracting station to the extraordinary commissioners for the execution of the planned interventions. Extraordinary Commissioners therefore derogate from the statutory provisions on public contracts and in this area only compliance with the provisions of the Anti-Mafia Laws Code and the prevention measures is ensured. Once again, I would ask you to consider the scope of the powers which derogate from the legal provisions on public contracts. So I think it is common sense at least to impose the justification for the derogations from the laws: let us make an extraordinary commissioner outlaw. This amendment merely introduces the obligation to state reasons. Moreover, I must point out that the opinion given by the Commission to the draft law that we are examining, as shown in the summary report of 9 May 2019, contains precisely this report to the relevant Commissions, which, however, have not taken account of it. I would expect for consistency a vote in favour at least of the members of the Justice Commission, who voted for that opinion.

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u493	GRASSI (M5S). Mr President, I would like to speak quickly with an explanation of vote on the amendment introducing a profile of the protection of the civil servant who is to take a decision to revoke any granting measures. I am referring, in particular, to motorway concessions, just to say the least. Remember that there has been a season in which in this country the supremacy of private action has been supported also in the management of goods and services of public importance. The intervention of the private - it should be remembered - in areas where for structural reasons it is impossible to create a free market creates, in reality, a monopoly regime. Paradigmatic example of the distorted use of private activity is precisely given by the motorway concessions and we all remember that the contracts that have been concluded - and it is a fact that has emerged also in the journalistic field - in the concessionary office contain real vexatory clauses to the detriment of the State. In these cases, the withdrawal of the concession, the resolution of the concessionary relationship is objectively for the benefit of the State, but you also understand that, precisely because of the wording of these contracts, the public officials are reluctant, concerned, intimidated, because they know well that they have on the other side of the large enterprises able, surely, to create a state that we could define as metus, that is, of fear in the action. In order to raise the public official called for in these measures, we have introduced this rule which provides for a prior review of the legality of the Court of Auditors on the official's act, i.e. the act of revocation. There is no legal inconsistency, because the Court of Auditors' review of the legality serves to exempt the official from liability. You are well aware that, despite the review of the legality of the Court of Auditors, although preventive, for the autonomy of judges and judicial orders, it may well be that a judgment of responsibility is reached separately. The public official must be protected, he must be left in a position to act with maximum serenity and this in order to achieve the best public interest. [[Applause from the M5S Group.]]

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u504	PAGE (FI-BP). Mr President, the subject of these proposals is that of the Gran Sasso tunnel, which has become the honour of the news recently. On the amendment of the rapporteurs - which however provides to intervene with the appointment of a commissioner - have been proposed, heard the President of the Region, and heard the technicians who will have to deal with the matter, a series of sub-amendments that tend essentially to consider the serious, important and imposing problems - because they also compete to the quality of the waters that supply the aqueducts that from Gran Sasso lead up to the entire Teraman coast - with a similar position in relation, for example, to the Morandi bridge. This means that the Commissioner who will have to be appointed to implement the necessary works to make possible all the work on the quality of the waters of Gran Sasso should also be paid and the economic resources should be placed in the best way to be able to do this. We therefore hope that the amendments will be accepted by the majority.

ParlaMint-IT_2019-06-05-LEG18-Senato-sed-118.ana.u509	DE PETRIS (Misto-LEU). Mr President, in view of the favourable opinion expressed on the previous sub-amendment by Senators Castaldi and Di Girolamo, I would have expected that the meaning of our sub-amendment should be considered - and thus understood. It concerns one of the issues on which several times, precisely on the case of the Gran Sasso and also with respect to the old experience of commissioning, there had been a commitment, among other things also reiterated in the discussion within the Abruzzo Region. I am referring to the participation in the information of citizens and, above all, to the involvement of the observatory, which in all these years has put with force all the critical environmental safety of the water basin. The sub-amendment is very simple: it calls for a representative of the observatory to be present in the steering and coordination booth. On the other hand, it addresses another issue and, within this rule on the police station (on which I have the impression that the old roads are already being re-established), it places absolute priority on the safety and protection of the aquifer basin, on the relocation of dangerous substances within the laboratories. This is one of the fundamental issues on which I would also have expected that in the amendment on the special Commissioner of Gran Sasso there would be a trace. The other issue is about resources. There's only 20 plus 100 million here. We all know for a very long time - they have been quantified - that there had been a commitment to at least be posted about 172 million, which were those related to minimum safety to start work. I am very amazed that the part on the observatory and not even the part on the indication of the relocation of dangerous substances within the physics laboratories have not been taken into account at all.
